\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{\prime}(U^{a}+\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}V_{n},\varphi^{a}+(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}\varPsi_{n})|_{x_{3}=0})(\delta V_{n},\delta\psi_{n})

\displaystyle-\mathbb{B}^{\prime}(U^{a}+V_{n+1/2},\varphi^{a}+(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}\varPsi_{n})|_{x_{3}=0})(\delta V_{n},\delta\psi_{n})={\mathbf{0}}.

Using (
6.35
)–(
6.37
), we deduce that
\hat{e}_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}={\mathbf{0}}
.
Thanks to (
6.36
), the error term
{e}_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{e}_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{L}^{\prime\prime}\big(U^{a}+V_{n+1/2}+\tau(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}V_{n}-V_{n+1/2}),\,\varPhi^{a}+\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}\varPsi_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\big((\delta V_{n},\delta\varPsi_{n}),(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}V_{n}-V_{n+1/2},0)\big){\rm d}\tau.

Apply the Sobolev embedding theorem, (
6.29
), (
6.33
), and (
6.38
), we find that

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{U}^{a},\,V_{n+1/2},\,\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}V_{n}-V_{n+1/2},\,\widetilde{\varPhi}^{a},\,\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}\varPsi_{n})\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon,

allowing us to use Proposition
6.2
for
\epsilon
suitably small.
Furthermore, from (
6.29
)–(
6.31
) and (
6.38
), we have

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{U}^{a},\,V_{n+1/2},\,\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}V_{n},\,\widetilde{\varPhi}^{a},\,\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{n}}\varPsi_{n})\|_{H^{m+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon\left(\theta_{n}^{(m+1-{\mu})_{+}+1}+\theta_{n}^{m+3-{\mu}}\right)

for
2\leq m\leq\tilde{\mu}-1
.
Using Proposition
6.2
, hypothesis
\mathrm{\bf H}_{n-1}
, and
(
6.38
), we obtain the estimate for term
{e}_{n}^{\prime\prime\prime},
thereby completing the proof of lemma.
∎

For the last error term (
6.16
),

\displaystyle D_{n+1/2}\delta\varPsi_{n}=\frac{\delta\varPsi_{n}}{\partial_{3}(\varPhi^{a}+\varPsi_{n+1/2})}R_{n},\quad\text{where }R_{n}:=\partial_{3}\mathbb{L}(U^{a}+V_{n+1/2},\varPhi^{a}+\varPsi_{n+1/2}),

we first observe that

\displaystyle|\partial_{3}(\varPhi^{a\pm}+\varPsi_{n+1/2}^{\pm})|\geq\frac{1}{2},

as deduced from (
5.14c
), (
6.36
), and (
6.33
)
for sufficiently small
\epsilon
.
Consequently, we arrive at the following lemma, analogous to
[
25
, Lemma 8.6]
or
[
12
, Lemma 12]
. The proof is omitted here for brevity.

Lemma 6.7
.

If
{\mu}\geq 5
and
\tilde{\mu}>{\mu}
,
then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
large enough such that

\displaystyle\|D_{n+1/2}\delta\varPsi_{n}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{n}^{\ell_{4}(m)-1}\Delta_{n}\quad\ \textrm{if }\ m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}-1,

(6.51)

for
n=0,\ldots,{N}-1
,
where

\ell_{4}(m):=\max\{(m+2-{\mu})_{+}+8-2{\mu},(m+1-{\mu})_{+}+9-2{\mu},m+6-2{\mu}\}.

Lemmas
6.3
–
6.7
provide the following estimates for
e_{n}
,
\tilde{e}_{n}
, and
\hat{e}_{n}

defined in (
6.17
) and (
6.19
).

Corollary 6.1
.

If
{\mu}\geq 5
and
\tilde{\mu}>{\mu}
, then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
large enough
such that

\displaystyle\|e_{n}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{n}^{\ell_{4}(m)-1}\Delta_{n}

\displaystyle\quad\ \textrm{if }\ m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}-1,

(6.52)

\displaystyle\|\hat{e}_{n}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{n}^{\ell_{2}(m)-1}\Delta_{n}

\displaystyle\quad\ \textrm{if }\ m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}-1,

(6.53)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{e}_{n}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{n}^{\ell_{2}(m)-1}\Delta_{n}

\displaystyle\quad\ \textrm{if }\ m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}-2,

(6.54)

for
n=0,\ldots,{N}-1,

where
\ell_{2}(m)
and
\ell_{4}(m)
are defined
in
Lemma
6.4
and
Lemma
6.7
, respectively.

6.5.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

We first prove the following lemma for
accumulated error terms
E_{n}
,
\widetilde{E}_{n}
, and
\hat{E}_{n}

that are defined in (
6.8
) and (
6.13
).

Lemma 6.8
.

If
{\mu}\geq 7
and
\tilde{\mu}={\mu}+3
, then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
large enough such that

\displaystyle\|E_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+2}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{{N}},

(6.55)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{E}_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}+\|\widehat{E}_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}.

(6.56)

Proof.

Following
[
25
,
12
]
, we first note that
\ell_{4}({\mu}+2)\leq 1
when
{\mu}\geq 7
.
From (
6.52
), one has

\displaystyle\|E_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+2}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq\sum_{n=0}^{{N}-1}\|e_{n}\|_{H^{{\mu}+2}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq\sum_{n=0}^{{N}-1}C\epsilon^{2}\Delta_{n}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{{N}},

for
{\mu}\geq 7
and
{\mu}+2\leq\tilde{\mu}-1
.
Since
\ell_{2}({\mu}+1)=6-{\mu}\leq-1
for
{\mu}\geq 7

and
{\mu}+1\leq\tilde{\mu}-2
,
from (
6.53
)–(
6.54
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{E}_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}+\|\widehat{E}_{{N}}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{{N}-1}\left\{\|\tilde{e}_{n}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}+\|\hat{e}_{n}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{{N}-1}C\epsilon^{2}\theta_{n}^{-2}\Delta_{n}\leq C\epsilon^{2},

where we have utilized (
6.9
) and (
6.30
)
to obtain the last inequality.
The minimal possible
\tilde{\mu}
is
{\mu}+3
.
The proof of the lemma is completed.
∎

Using the lemma above, we have the estimates for

f_{{N}}
,
g_{{N}}
,
and
h_{{N}}^{\pm}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

If
{\mu}\geq 7
and
\tilde{\mu}={\mu}+3
, then there exist
\epsilon>0
suitably small and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
large enough such that

\displaystyle\|f_{{N}}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\Delta_{{N}}\left\{\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}\left(\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\epsilon^{2}\right)+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{{N}}^{\ell_{4}(m)-1}\right\},

(6.57)

\displaystyle\|g_{{N}}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\Delta_{{N}}\big(\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}+\theta_{{N}}^{\ell_{2}(m)-1}\big),

(6.58)

for
m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}+1
,
and

\displaystyle\|h_{{N}}^{\pm}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\Delta_{{N}}\big(\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}+\theta_{{N}}^{\ell_{2}(m)-1}\big)\quad\ \textrm{for }\ m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}.

(6.59)

Proof.

Using
\theta_{{N}-1}\leq\theta_{{N}}\leq\sqrt{2}\theta_{{N}-1}

and
\Delta_{{N}-1}\leq 3\Delta_{{N}}
,
from (
6.1a
), (
6.1c
), (
6.52
), and (
6.55
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{N}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}-\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}-1}})f^{a}-(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}-\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}-1}})E_{{N}-1}-\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}e_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{{N}}\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}\big(\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\theta_{{N}}^{-1}\|E_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{{\mu}+2}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\big)+\|\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}e_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{{N}}\left\{\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}(\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\epsilon^{2})+\epsilon^{2}\theta_{{N}}^{\ell_{4}(m)-1}\right\}.

By using (
6.54
) and (
6.56
), we get

\displaystyle\|g_{N}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}-\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}-1}})\widetilde{E}_{{N}-1}-\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}\tilde{e}_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Delta_{{N}}\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}\|\widetilde{E}_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\|\mathcal{S}_{\theta_{{N}}}\tilde{e}_{{N}-1}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{2}\Delta_{{N}}\big(\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-2}+\theta_{{N}}^{\ell_{2}(m)-1}\big).

Similarly, we can obtain (
6.59
) for
h_{N}^{\pm}
from (
6.53
)
and (
6.56
). The proof of Lemma
6.9
is completed.
∎

In the following lemma, we obtain the estimate of differences

\delta V_{{N}},

\delta\varPsi_{{N}}
, and

\delta\psi_{{N}},
by using the tame estimate (
4.19
).
See
[
25
, Lemma 16]

or
[
12
, Lemma 8.10]
for the proof.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
{\mu}\geq 7
and
\tilde{\mu}={\mu}+3
.
If
\epsilon>0
and
\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}/\epsilon
are suitably small
and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
is large enough, then

\displaystyle\|(\delta V_{{N}},\delta\varPsi_{{N}})\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\|\delta\psi_{{N}}\|_{H^{m+1}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}\leq\epsilon\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-1}\Delta_{{N}}\quad\textrm{for $m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}$.}

(6.60)

Lemma
6.10
establishes the first part of the hypothesis
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{N}}
.
The following lemma addresses the remaining components of
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{N}}
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
{\mu}\geq 7
and
\tilde{\mu}={\mu}+3
.
If
\epsilon>0
and
\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}/\epsilon
are suitably small
and
\theta_{0}\geq 1
is large enough,
then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}(V_{{N}},\varPsi_{{N}})-f^{a}\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq 2\epsilon\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\textrm{for }m=2,\ldots,\tilde{\mu}-1,

(6.61)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}(V_{{N}},\psi_{{N}})\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}\leq\epsilon\theta_{{N}}^{m-{\mu}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\textrm{for }m=3,\ldots,{{\mu}},

(6.62)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}(V_{{N}},\varPsi_{{N}})\|_{H^{3}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}\leq\epsilon\theta_{{N}}^{2-{\mu}}.

(6.63)

We refer to
[
25
, Lemmas 17–18]

or
[
12
, Lemma 8.11]
for the proof of Lemma
6.11
.
Let us assume
{\mu}\geq 7
,
\tilde{\mu}={\mu}+3
,

\epsilon>0
and
\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}/\epsilon
sufficiently small. Additionally, let
\theta_{0}\geq 1
be large enough to satisfy
the assumptions of Lemmas
6.10
–
6.11
,
from which the inductive hypothesis
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{N}}
follows.
Then, as shown in
[
25
, Lemma 19]
or

[
12
, Lemma 8.12]
, it can be proved that the hypothesis
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{0}}
holds.

Lemma 6.12
.

If
\|f^{a}\|_{H^{{\mu}+1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}/\epsilon
is small enough,
then the hypothesis
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{0}}
holds.

With this, we are ready to complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
.
Our proof closely follows the idea in
[
25
,
12
]
, but is included here for completeness.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
. Let
\tilde{\mu}:=s_{0}-4\geq 10
and
{\mu}:=\tilde{\mu}-3\geq 7
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, the initial data
(U_{0}^{\pm},\varphi_{0})
are compatible up to order
s_{0}=\tilde{\mu}+4
.
If
(\tilde{U}_{0}^{\pm},\varphi_{0})
is sufficiently small
in
H^{s_{0}+1/2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\times H^{s_{0}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
where
\widetilde{U}_{0}^{\pm}:=U_{0}^{\pm}-\bar{U}^{\pm}
,
then the assumption (
6.29
) and all the requirements
of Lemmas
6.10
–
6.12
are satisfied,
due to (
5.16
) and (
5.19
). Thus,
\mathrm{\bf H}_{{N}}
holds
for all
{N}\in\mathbb{N}
. From the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\|(\delta V_{n},\delta\varPsi_{n})\|_{H^{m}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})}+\|\delta\psi_{n}\|_{H^{m+1}_{\gamma}(\omega_{T})}\right)\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\theta_{n}^{m-{\mu}-2}<\infty,\quad 3\leq m\leq{\mu}-1,

we conclude that
(V_{n},\varPsi_{n})
converges
to some
(V,\varPsi)
in
H^{{\mu}-1}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})
,
and
\psi_{n}
converges to some
\psi
in
H^{{\mu}}_{\gamma}(\Omega_{T})
.
Taking the limit in (
6.61
)–(
6.62
) for
m={\mu}-1=s_{0}-8
,
and in (
6.63
), we obtain that
(V,\varPsi)
satisfies (
5.20
).
Consequently,
(U,\varPhi)=(U^{a}+V,\varPhi^{a}+\varPsi)
is a solution to (
2.6
)–(
2.11
) in
\Omega_{T}^{+}
.
The proof of Theorem
2.1
is complete.
∎
